## Gemini:

> 7/20/2026 8:31:13

Seeing it rendered from above like this makes it clear. The internal partitions are placing texturing cleanly, but the ceiling and floor surfaces are completely black, which makes the whole space look like a void from above.

This happens because the floor and ceiling planes are currently defined as a thin $13.6 \times 15.6$ surface inside the geometry math. When viewed from above, the outer boundary walls (which span to a width of $14.0 \times 16.0$) completely hide the textured edges, or the ceiling material color is absorbing all the top-down diagnostic light.

To fix the void look and make the gallery clean from a top-down view, we need to match the floor and ceiling planes to the absolute outer edges of your boundary framework.

Here are the values to update inside your `generate_apartment_layout()` function:

### 1. The Geometry Fix
Find the `_add_plane` calls inside `ApartmentBuilder.gd` and change their sizing dimensions to match the absolute exterior size ($14.0$ and $16.0$):

`​`​`gdscript
	# Bake clean ground floors and ceilings to match the full external layout
	_add_plane(building_root, Vector3(0.0, 0.05, 0.0), Vector3(14.0, 0.1, 16.0), floor_mat)
	_add_plane(building_root, Vector3(0.0, 2.95, 0.0), Vector3(14.0, 0.1, 16.0), ceiling_mat)
`​`​`

### 2. Make the Ceiling Transparent from Above (Optional)
If you want to look down *through* the ceiling while flying around in noclip without it blocking your view, you can set the material to only render its inner faces. Add this rule where `ceiling_mat` is initialized:

`​`​`gdscript
	var ceiling_mat = StandardMaterial3D.new()
	ceiling_mat.albedo_color = Color(0.13, 0.13, 0.13)
	ceiling_mat.roughness = 0.95
	# Backlight/cull mode makes the ceiling invisible from the outside/above, but solid from inside
	ceiling_mat.cull_mode = BaseMaterial3D.CULL_FRONT
`​`​`

Making these two tweaks will completely square off the edges of your structural template so the black gaps vanish when checking the floor layout.

## User:

> 7/20/2026 8:33:54

you broke my wasd mm ovement, also give me X for duck or down in noclip

## Gemini:

> 7/20/2026 8:33:54

Ah, my bad, Scott. The WASD movement broke because `Input.get_vector()` relies on specific action names that might not be set up standard in your input map for those keys, or they conflict with the built-in UI mappings. Let's revert the movement checks back to explicit keycodes so WASD works flawlessly no matter what your input map looks like. 

I've also added **X** for ducking (when on the ground) and flying down (when in Noclip), while keeping **Ctrl** as an alternative down button.

### Updated PlayerController.gd

Replace your entire `PlayerController.gd` script with this layout:

`​`​`gdscript
extends CharacterBody3D

@export var walk_speed: float = 4.0
@export var run_speed: float = 7.0
@export var jump_velocity: float = 4.5
@export var mouse_sensitivity: float = 0.002

# Noclip & Crouch variables
var is_noclip: bool = false
@export var noclip_speed: float = 10.0
var is_crouching: bool = false
var normal_height: float = 1.8
var crouch_height: float = 0.9

# Gravity from project settings
var gravity: float = ProjectSettings.get_setting("physics/3d/default_gravity")

@onready var head = $HeadAssembly
@onready var camera = $HeadAssembly/PlayerCamera
@onready var collision = $PlayerCollision

func _ready() -> void:
	Input.mouse_mode = Input.MOUSE_MODE_CAPTURED

func _unhandled_input(event: InputEvent) -> void:
	# ESCAPE: Toggle mouse lock/release for developer safety
	if event.is_action_pressed("ui_cancel") or (event is InputEventKey and event.keycode == KEY_ESCAPE and event.pressed):
		if Input.mouse_mode == Input.MOUSE_MODE_CAPTURED:
			Input.mouse_mode = Input.MOUSE_MODE_VISIBLE
		else:
			Input.mouse_mode = Input.MOUSE_MODE_CAPTURED
			
	# V KEY: Toggle Noclip mode
	if event is InputEventKey and event.keycode == KEY_V and event.pressed:
		is_noclip = !is_noclip
		collision.disabled = is_noclip
		velocity = Vector3.ZERO
		# Reset height if toggling out of noclip while crouching
		if not is_noclip:
			collision.shape.height = normal_height
		print("Noclip mode: ", "ON" if is_noclip else "OFF")

	# Mouse Look Logic
	if Input.mouse_mode == Input.MOUSE_MODE_CAPTURED and event is InputEventMouseMotion:
		rotate_y(-event.relative.x * mouse_sensitivity)
		head.rotate_x(-event.relative.y * mouse_sensitivity)
		head.rotation.x = clamp(head.rotation.x, deg_to_rad(-89), deg_to_rad(89))

func _physics_process(delta: float) -> void:
	if is_noclip:
		_process_noclip_movement(delta)
	else:
		_process_standard_movement(delta)

func _process_standard_movement(delta: float) -> void:
	# Add gravity
	if not is_on_floor():
		velocity.y -= gravity * delta

	# Handle Jump via Spacebar
	if Input.is_key_pressed(KEY_SPACE) and is_on_floor():
		velocity.y = jump_velocity

	# Handle Ducking via X Key
	if Input.is_key_pressed(KEY_X):
		is_crouching = true
		collision.shape.height = crouch_height
		head.position.y = 0.9 # Drop head assembly lower
	else:
		is_crouching = false
		collision.shape.height = normal_height
		head.position.y = 1.6 # Restore normal head height

	# Determine speed (Shift to run)
	var current_speed = run_speed if Input.is_key_pressed(KEY_SHIFT) else walk_speed
	if is_crouching:
		current_speed *= 0.5 # Slow down while ducking

	# Hardcoded WASD Vector Mapping
	var forward_back = 0.0
	var left_right = 0.0
	
	if Input.is_key_pressed(KEY_W): forward_back -= 1.0
	if Input.is_key_pressed(KEY_S): forward_back += 1.0
	if Input.is_key_pressed(KEY_A): left_right -= 1.0
	if Input.is_key_pressed(KEY_D): left_right += 1.0

	# Get input direction relative to player rotation
	var direction = (transform.basis * Vector3(left_right, 0, forward_back)).normalized()
	
	if direction:
		velocity.x = direction.x * current_speed
		velocity.z = direction.z * current_speed
	else:
		velocity.x = move_toward(velocity.x, 0, current_speed)
		velocity.z = move_toward(velocity.z, 0, current_speed)

	move_and_slide()

func _process_noclip_movement(delta: float) -> void:
	var forward_back = 0.0
	var left_right = 0.0
	
	if Input.is_key_pressed(KEY_W): forward_back -= 1.0
	if Input.is_key_pressed(KEY_S): forward_back += 1.0
	if Input.is_key_pressed(KEY_A): left_right -= 1.0
	if Input.is_key_pressed(KEY_D): left_right += 1.0
	
	# Compute direction based exactly on camera direction (3D flight vector)
	var direction = (camera.global_transform.basis * Vector3(left_right, 0, forward_back)).normalized()
	
	# Spacebar to fly UP, X or CTRL to fly DOWN
	var vertical_dir = 0.0
	if Input.is_key_pressed(KEY_SPACE):
		vertical_dir += 1.0
	if Input.is_key_pressed(KEY_X) or Input.is_key_pressed(KEY_CTRL):
		vertical_dir -= 1.0
		
	if direction or vertical_dir != 0.0:
		velocity = direction * noclip_speed
		velocity.y += vertical_dir * noclip_speed
	else:
		velocity = velocity.move_toward(Vector3.ZERO, noclip_speed * 0.2)
		
	# Global translation bypasses character body physics step entirely
	global_position += velocity * delta
`​`​`

### Controls Update:
* **`W` / `A` / `S` / `D`**: Direct keyboard tracking for movement.
* **`Spacebar`**: Jump (Normal) / Fly Up (Noclip).
* **`X`**: Duck / Crouch (Normal) / Fly Down (Noclip).
* **`Ctrl`**: Alternative Fly Down (Noclip).

`​`​`gdscript

	{"pos": Vector3(-6.7, 1.5, 0.0), "dims": Vector3(0.2, 3.0, 15.6), "texture": "wall2.png", "has_door": false, "has_window": true, "window_pos": Vector3(-6.7, 1.3, 1.0), "window_dims": Vector3(0.4, 1.2, 2.5)},

	# --- INTERNAL ROOM PARTITIONS ---
	# Horizontal wall dividing Mom's Room/Closet (A1/AZ) from Sarah's Room & Hallway
	{"pos": Vector3(-1.0, 1.5, -3.5), "dims": Vector3(11.4, 3.0, 0.2), "texture": "wall2.png", "has_door": true, "door_pos": Vector3(-4.0, 1.0, -3.5), "door_dims": Vector3(1.2, 2.1, 0.4), "has_window": false},
	# Vertical divider between Mom's Bedroom (A1) and Closet (AZ)

	# Long vertical spine separating Hallway (H) from Living Room (LIV) and Kitchen (KIT)
	{"pos": Vector3(1.5, 1.5, 2.1), "dims": Vector3(0.2, 3.0, 11.2), "texture": "wall1.png", "has_door": false, "has_window": false},
	# Horizontal wall dividing Living Room (LIV) from Kitchen (KIT)

]

### The Open-Concept Floor Split Logic

To cleanly break the floor textures under the living area without using a solid partition wall, swap out the basic floor generation segment in your `generate_apartment_layout()` function with this two-part split mesh block:

`​`​`gdscript
	# --- ENVIRONMENT SURFACES (OPEN CONCEPT FLOOR SPLIT) ---
	var living_floor_mat = _load_horror_material("floor1.png", Color(0.1, 0.07, 0.05), Vector3(6.0, 6.0, 6.0), 0.85)
	
	# Load a secondary texture asset if it exists for the kitchen tiling, fallback to cold concrete gray
	var kitchen_floor_mat = _load_horror_material("wall4.png", Color(0.2, 0.2, 0.2), Vector3(4.0, 4.0, 4.0), 0.5) 
	
	var ceiling_mat = StandardMaterial3D.new()
	ceiling_mat.albedo_color = Color(0.13, 0.13, 0.13)
	ceiling_mat.roughness = 0.95
	
	# 1. Main Living Area Floor Plan Segment (Covers front wall up to the Z=5.5 threshold boundary)
	_add_plane(building_root, Vector3(2.6, 0.05, -1.0), Vector3(8.2, 0.1, 13.0), living_floor_mat)
	
	# 2. Kitchen Dedicated Floor Segment (Covers back partition wall past Z=5.5)
	_add_plane(building_root, Vector3(2.6, 0.05, 6.6), Vector3(8.2, 0.1, 2.2), kitchen_floor_mat)
	
	# 3. Rest of House Core Base Flooring
	_add_plane(building_root, Vector3(-2.6, 0.05, 0.0), Vector3(5.8, 0.1, 15.6), living_floor_mat)
	
	# Absolute ceiling bounds cover tracking overhead
	_add_plane(building_root, Vector3(0.0, 2.95, 0.0), Vector3(14.0, 0.1, 16.0), ceiling_mat)
`​`​`

## Gemini:

> 7/20/2026 8:55:09

The error is popping up because the loop is trying to read `"has_window"` from every entry in `wall_gallery`, but a couple of the inner partition walls don't have that key defined in their dictionaries. GDScript throws a fit when it tries to access a key that isn't there.

You can fix this instantly without changing your layout configuration by using the dictionary's `.get()` method inside the loop. `.get()` allows you to look for a key and safely fallback to `false` if it's missing.

Open `ApartmentBuilder.gd`, scroll down to line 94 inside the `generate_apartment_layout()` loop, and update the checks like this:

`​`​`gdscript
		# Process interior doors bound straight to the config array coordinates
		if wall_data.get("has_door", false):
			var door_cut = CSGBox3D.new()
			door_cut.operation = CSGShape3D.OPERATION_SUBTRACTION
			door_cut.position = wall_data["door_pos"]
			door_cut.size = wall_data["door_dims"]
			building_root.add_child(door_cut)
			
		# Process window punches bound explicitly inside this wall block instance
		if wall_data.get("has_window", false):
			_punch_absolute_window(building_root, wall_data["window_pos"], wall_data["window_dims"], glass_mat)
`​`​`

### Why this fixes it:
* Changing `wall_data["has_window"]` to `wall_data.get("has_window", false)` tells Godot: *"Look for 'has_window'. If you don't find it, just assume it's false and keep moving."*
* This completely prevents the crash and lets your custom room layout build cleanly.

## Gemini:

> 7/20/2026 8:56:29

If you are still seeing black walls, it means the global diagnostic lighting isn't hitting the vertical faces of the interior meshes properly, or the default ambient lighting in your environment is pitching them into total shadow. 

Let's drop a localized `OmniLight3D` into the center of every single room zone based on your exact blueprint proportions. This will blast the textures with light from the inside out and completely eliminate the shadows.

### The Lighting Code Solution

Scroll to the bottom of your `generate_apartment_layout()` function in `ApartmentBuilder.gd`, remove the old `DirectionalLight3D` block, and drop in this clean room-by-room lighting array layout:

`​`​`gdscript
	# --- HORROR ROOM LIGHTING SUITE ---
	# Array mapping light placements directly to the center of every room zone
	var room_lights: Array = [
		{"name": "Mom_Bedroom_Light", "pos": Vector3(2.5, 2.3, -5.6), "color": Color(0.9, 0.85, 0.75), "energy": 1.5, "range": 8.0},
		{"name": "Mom_Closet_Light",  "pos": Vector3(-4.5, 2.3, -5.6), "color": Color(0.85, 0.85, 0.9), "energy": 1.0, "range": 5.0},
		{"name": "Sarah_Bedroom_Light","pos": Vector3(-4.5, 2.3, -0.5), "color": Color(0.9, 0.8, 0.8), "energy": 1.5, "range": 8.0},
		{"name": "Sarah_Closet_Light", "pos": Vector3(-2.6, 2.3, 1.5), "color": Color(0.7, 0.7, 0.8), "energy": 0.8, "range": 4.0},
		{"name": "Bathroom_Light",    "pos": Vector3(-5.1, 2.3, 6.1), "color": Color(0.75, 0.9, 0.8), "energy": 1.2, "range": 6.0},
		{"name": "Main_Hallway_Light", "pos": Vector3(-0.5, 2.3, 2.0), "color": Color(0.85, 0.8, 0.7), "energy": 1.2, "range": 7.0},
		{"name": "Living_Room_Light",  "pos": Vector3(4.0, 2.3, 1.0), "color": Color(0.95, 0.9, 0.8), "energy": 2.0, "range": 10.0},
		{"name": "Kitchen_Light",      "pos": Vector3(4.0, 2.3, 6.5), "color": Color(0.9, 0.95, 1.0), "energy": 1.8, "range": 8.0}
	]

	for light_data in room_lights:
		var light = OmniLight3D.new()
		light.name = light_data["name"]
		light.position = light_data["pos"]
		light.light_color = light_data["color"]
		light.light_energy = light_data["energy"]
		light.omni_range = light_data["range"]
		
		# Enables clean shadows so internal walls cast realistic geometry blockades
		light.shadow_enabled = true
		light.shadow_blur = 1.5
		
		add_child(light)

	print("Lighting Suite Baked: 8 internal zone omni-lights active.")
`​`​`

### Why this fixes the black walls:
* **Positioning:** The lights are suspended at $Y = 2.3$ meters, placing them perfectly inside each room right below the $2.95$-meter ceiling.
* **Omni Emission:** Unlike directional lights that hit at a single angle, `OmniLight3D` shoots light in all $360^{\circ}$ vectors simultaneously, ensuring every side of your modular partitions gets fully illuminated.

I see exactly what went wrong. When we converted the doorway thresholds into wide open structural arches for the hallway portals, the subtraction values got shifted. That narrow, dark green pillar sticking right down the middle of the archway is actually a leftover piece of the original wall segment because the cutout size didn't span wide enough to erase it entirely.

To clear that rogue pillar out of your hallway entrance, we need to widen the `door_dims` and match its `door_pos` right on top of that specific partition line.

Open `ApartmentBuilder.gd` and find the **Mom's Suite / Hallway divider** entry inside the `wall_gallery` array. Replace that single line entry with this corrected version:

`​`​`gdscript

	{"pos": Vector3(-1.0, 1.5, -3.5), "dims": Vector3(11.4, 3.0, 0.2), "texture": "wall2.png", "has_door": true, "door_pos": Vector3(-1.0, 1.5, -3.5), "door_dims": Vector3(3.5, 3.1, 0.4), "has_window": false},
`​`​`

### What this changes:
* **`door_pos` aligned to `pos`:** By setting the door cutout's local X position directly to `-1.0` (matching the wall itself), the CSG cutting tool centers perfectly on the hallway threshold.
* **Widened `door_dims`:** Increasing the cut width to `3.5` ensures it cleanly shears off the entire wall section block intersecting the hallway lane, completely removing the slice down the middle.

## Gemini:

> 7/20/2026 9:03:19

Alright, look at where that cutout is happening. The stray pillar is gone, but now the archway is shifted too far to the left, leaving the right side of the hallway blocked off by a floating section of the wall segment, and the bedroom entry next to it looks misaligned.

The issue is that `door_pos` coordinates in CSG operate relative to the global origin if they are added directly to the `building_root`, rather than being localized to the wall's position itself. Because the horizontal wall has an X position of `-1.0` and a width of `11.4`, centering the cut at `-1.0` is missing the actual visual gap of the hallway lane.

Let's fix the alignment strictly by adjusting the X position and expanding the cutout width of that portal so it cleanly flushes with the hallway spine.

Open `ApartmentBuilder.gd` and swap out that horizontal wall configuration line inside `wall_gallery` with this precisely positioned one:

`​`​`gdscript
	# Horizontal wall dividing Mom's Suite from Hallway - Adjusted X position and width to completely clear the hallway lane

## Gemini:

Here are the texture and shader resources we looked at for setting up the environment layouts:

*   **AmbientCG** (`ambientcg.com`) – Clean, public domain (CC0) PBR materials. Excellent for high-resolution tiling textures like `bathtile.png`, `kittile.png`, and structural concrete/wood bases.
*   **Godot Shaders** (`godotshaders.com`) – The largest community repository for Godot-specific visual shaders. Great if you want to apply custom canvas effects, screen-space horror grain, or unique vertex distortions to your modular walls. 

Both sites let you drop resources directly into your Python asset pipeline or load them dynamically straight inside your GDScript configurations.

> 7/20/2026 9:11:22

shader_type spatial;
render_mode cull_disabled;

// Base gradient
uniform vec3 bottom_color : source_color;
uniform vec3 top_color : source_color;

// Color variations and noise
uniform vec3 color_variation_1 : source_color;
uniform sampler2D noise_variation_1;
uniform float Noise1Scale = 20.0;

uniform vec3 color_variation_2 : source_color;
uniform sampler2D noise_variation_2;
uniform float Noise2Scale = 20.0;

// Wind & displacement
uniform float windSpeed = 0.005;
uniform float windDis = 0.2;
uniform float noiseStrength = 0.5;
uniform float displaceStrength = 3.0;

// Wind noise texture
uniform sampler2D wind_noise;
uniform float windNoiseScale = 20.0;
uniform vec2 windNoisePanSpeed = vec2(0.03, 0.03);
uniform float noiseFloor = 0.3;
uniform float wind_noise_scale_strength = 0.2;

// Wind Shadow
uniform vec3 wind_shadow_color : source_color = vec3(0.0, 0.2, 0.0);
uniform float wind_shadow_strength = 0.4;
uniform float flatten_shadow_strength = 0.7;
uniform float wind_shadow_displacement_threshold = 0.15;
uniform float wind_shadow_smoothing = 0.3;

// Flattening
uniform vec3 interracting_object_pos = vec3(0.0, 0.0, 0.0);
uniform float flatten_radius = 0.75;
uniform float flatten_strength = 3.0;
uniform float flatten_floor = 0.3;

// Noise-based vertical scale
uniform float combined_noise_min_scale = 0.5;
uniform float combined_noise_max_scale = 1.5;
uniform bool invert_combined_noise = false;

// Debug toggles
uniform bool debug_show_wind_noise = false;
uniform bool debug_show_combined_noise = false;

varying vec3 worldPos;
varying float wind_displacement_strength;
varying float flatten_amount_for_overlay;
varying float combinedNoiseDebug;

void vertex() {
    worldPos = (MODEL_MATRIX * vec4(VERTEX, 1.0)).xyz;
    vec3 instance_world_origin = (MODEL_MATRIX * vec4(0.0, 0.0, 0.0, 1.0)).xyz;

    // Combined noise from two textures
    vec2 uv1 = worldPos.xz / Noise1Scale;
    vec2 uv2 = worldPos.xz / Noise2Scale;
    float noise1 = texture(noise_variation_1, uv1).r;
    float noise2 = texture(noise_variation_2, uv2).r;
    float combinedNoise = clamp((0.5 + noise1) * noise2, 0.0, 1.0);
    if (invert_combined_noise) {
        combinedNoise = 1.0 - combinedNoise;
    }
    combinedNoiseDebug = combinedNoise;

    // Panning wind noise for vertical scale influence
    vec2 windNoiseUV = instance_world_origin.xz / windNoiseScale + windNoisePanSpeed * TIME;
    float windNoiseVal = texture(wind_noise, windNoiseUV).r;
    float windVerticalOffset = mix(-wind_noise_scale_strength, wind_noise_scale_strength, windNoiseVal);

    // Final noise-based vertical scale
    float verticalScaleNoise = mix(combined_noise_min_scale, combined_noise_max_scale, 1.0 - combinedNoise);
    float finalVerticalScale = verticalScaleNoise + windVerticalOffset;

    // Flattening
    float distToImpact = distance(worldPos, interracting_object_pos);
    float flattenFactor = clamp(1.0 - (distToImpact / flatten_radius), 0.0, 1.0);
    float flattenAmount = flattenFactor * flatten_strength;
    flatten_amount_for_overlay = flattenAmount;

    float flattenScale = mix(1.0, flatten_floor, flattenAmount);
    finalVerticalScale *= flattenScale;
    VERTEX.y *= finalVerticalScale;

    // Wind sway
    float shapedNoise = 0.0;
    float noiseVal = texture(wind_noise, windNoiseUV).r;
    float bias = noiseVal - 0.5;
    if (bias < 0.0) {
        shapedNoise = mix(0.0, noiseFloor - 0.5, -bias * 2.0);
    } else {
        shapedNoise = bias * bias * 2.0;
    }

    float instanceWindSpeed = max(0.0, windSpeed * (1.0 - shapedNoise * noiseStrength));
    float windResponse = 1.0 - flattenAmount;
    float instanceWindDis = windDis * (1.0 + shapedNoise * displaceStrength) * windResponse;
    wind_displacement_strength = instanceWindDis;

    float sineValue = sin(TIME * instanceWindSpeed);
    float heightFactor = clamp(UV.y, 0.0, 1.0);

    vec2 windDir = normalize(windNoisePanSpeed);
    if (length(windNoisePanSpeed) < 0.001) {
        windDir = vec2(1.0, 0.0);
    }

    vec2 sway = windDir * sineValue * instanceWindDis * (1.0 - heightFactor);
    VERTEX.x += sway.x;
    VERTEX.z += sway.y;
}

void fragment() {
    float heightFactor = clamp(UV.y, 0.0, 1.0);

    if (debug_show_combined_noise) {
        ALBEDO = vec3(combinedNoiseDebug);
    } else if (debug_show_wind_noise) {
        vec2 noiseUV = worldPos.xz / windNoiseScale + windNoisePanSpeed * TIME;
        float noiseVal = texture(wind_noise, noiseUV).r;
        ALBEDO = vec3(noiseVal);
    } else {
        vec3 baseGradient = mix(top_color, bottom_color, heightFactor);

        vec3 altGradient1 = mix(color_variation_1, bottom_color, heightFactor);
        float blendVal1 = texture(noise_variation_1, worldPos.xz / Noise1Scale).r;
        vec3 firstBlend = mix(baseGradient, altGradient1, blendVal1);

        vec3 altGradient2 = mix(color_variation_2, bottom_color, heightFactor);
        float blendVal2 = texture(noise_variation_2, worldPos.xz / Noise2Scale).r;
        vec3 finalBlend = mix(firstBlend, altGradient2, blendVal2);

        float noiseFactor = texture(wind_noise, worldPos.xz / windNoiseScale).r;
        finalBlend *= mix(0.9, 1.1, noiseFactor);

        float displace_fade = smoothstep(
            wind_shadow_displacement_threshold,
            wind_shadow_displacement_threshold + wind_shadow_smoothing,
            wind_displacement_strength
        );

        float flatten_fade = smoothstep(0.0, 1.0, flatten_amount_for_overlay);
        float total_overlay_strength = clamp(
            displace_fade * wind_shadow_strength +
            flatten_fade * flatten_shadow_strength,
            0.0, 1.0
        );

        vec3 tint = mix(vec3(1.0), wind_shadow_color, total_overlay_strength);
        finalBlend = mix(finalBlend, finalBlend * tint, total_overlay_strength);

        ALBEDO = finalBlend;
    }

    if (!FRONT_FACING) {
        NORMAL = -NORMAL;
    }
}

## Gemini:

> 7/20/2026 9:11:22

This is a solid, feature-rich grass shader. It handles a lot of the heavy lifting needed for a proper stylized horror or atmosphere-heavy environment—especially with the built-in object interaction/flattening support and dual-noise color variations.

Since you're developing on a Windows 10 machine utilizing **integrated/on-board video only (no dedicated GPU)**, running heavy grass simulations can absolutely tank your frame rates if it isn't optimized. 

Here is a performance optimization review for this specific shader to make sure it runs smoothly on your setup:

### Performance & GPU Optimization Tips

*   **Move Fragment Calculations to Vertex:** Right now, texture lookups for color blending (`blendVal1`, `blendVal2`, and `noiseFactor`) are happening inside the `fragment()` function. Because the fragment shader runs per-pixel rather than per-vertex, doing five texture fetches per pixel on an integrated GPU will choke fill-rate quickly. You should pass those noise values from the `vertex()` function to the `fragment()` function using `varying` variables.
*   **Shadow Casting:** In your Godot scene, make sure the mesh instances using this material have **Cast Shadows** turned *Off* (or set to *Shadows Only* on a few select clusters). Rendering real-time dynamic shadows on thousands of grass blades will instantly bottleneck your hardware.
*   **Keep the Geometry Lean:** Ensure the grass blade meshes themselves have very low vertex counts (e.g., a simple 3-quad strip or a cross-plane triangle layout). 

### How to implement the Vertex optimization:

To pass the data cleanly and save performance, add these new varyings at the top of the shader:
`​`​`glsl
varying float v_blendVal1;
varying float v_blendVal2;
varying float v_noiseFactor;
`​`​`

Then, calculate them once in the `vertex()` function:
`​`​`glsl
v_blendVal1 = texture(noise_variation_1, uv1).r;
v_blendVal2 = texture(noise_variation_2, uv2).r;
v_noiseFactor = texture(wind_noise, worldPos.xz / windNoiseScale).r;
`​`​`

Finally, use those `v_` variables in the `fragment()` function instead of calling `texture()` again. This will give you a massive performance boost on your hardware configuration.

## Gemini:

> 7/20/2026 9:12:02

We can handle the setup entirely within your existing `ApartmentBuilder.gd` script without needing to install any new plugins or complex tools. Since your project generates everything procedurally using Godot's built-in nodes, adding new logic just means writing a few lines of clean GDScript.

Here is how you can implement the player tracking script to drive the trampling effect, along with exactly how to hook it up.

### 1. Update your Player Script
To let the grass shader know where you are standing, the player needs to constantly send its position to the material. Open your `PlayerController.gd` script and add this line inside the `_physics_process` function right after your movement code:

`​`​`gdscript
# Send player position to the grass material for real-time trampling
# Assumes your grass mesh node is named "GrassMesh" inside the scene
var grass_mesh = get_node_or_null("../GrassMesh")
if grass_mesh and grass_mesh.material_override:
	grass_mesh.material_override.set_shader_parameter("interracting_object_pos", global_position)
`​`​`

### 2. Setting Up the Material in the Editor
You don't need an installer for shaders. Godot handles `.gdshader` files natively:

* **Create the Shader file**: Right-click in your Godot FileSystem dock, select **New -> Resource**, choose **Shader**, and name it `grass.gdshader`. Double-click it and paste the stylized grass shader code you found into the bottom panel.
* **Apply to the Mesh**: Select your grass mesh node (or a `MultiMeshInstance3D` if you are scattering thousands of blades). 
* **Create the Material**: In the Inspector, look for **Material Override**, click the dropdown, and select **New ShaderMaterial**.
* **Link the Shader**: Click into that new material, drag your `grass.gdshader` file into the **Shader** slot, and drop your noise textures into the uniform slots that appear below it.

### 3. Optimization Checklist for Your System
Because you are running on integrated graphics without a dedicated GPU, a few adjustments will keep your frame rates high:

* **Disable Cast Shadows**: In the Inspector for your grass mesh, scroll down to **Geometry** and set **Cast Shadow** to **Off**. Dynamic shadow rendering is the heaviest performance killer for dense vegetation.
* **Keep Vertex Count Low**: Ensure the base mesh asset for a single blade or cluster of grass is incredibly simple—ideally just 2 to 4 triangles. 
* **Use MultiMeshInstance3D**: Instead of placing individual grass mesh nodes manually, use a `MultiMeshInstance3D` node. It processes all the grass instances in a single optimized draw call, which protects your CPU from getting bottlenecked.

## Gemini:

> 7/20/2026 9:16:49

Let’s slow it down and do this step-by-step. Don't worry about coding anything right now; we are just clicking a few things in the Godot editor interface to link the shader to your actual 3D objects. 

Here is exactly how to do it without getting lost:

### Step 1: Open Your Scene
1. Look at your **Scene Tree panel** (usually on the top left of the screen).
2. Click on the 3D node that is supposed to be your grass. (If you are using a placeholder cube or plane for testing, click that).

---

### Step 2: Find the Material Override Slot
1. With your grass node selected, look over at the **Inspector panel** on the right side of your screen.
2. Scroll down until you see the **Geometry** section and click it to unfold it.
3. Look for the property labeled **Material Override** (it will say `[Empty]` right next to it).

---

### Step 3: Create the New Material
1. Click the `[Empty]` box next to **Material Override**.
2. A dropdown menu will pop up. Select **New ShaderMaterial**.
3. The box will turn green and say `ShaderMaterial`. **Click directly on that green box** to open up its properties.

---

### Step 4: Load the Shader File
1. Inside the properties that just opened up, you will see a slot labeled **Shader** (it will also say `[Empty]`).
2. Go to your **FileSystem panel** (usually bottom left) where your project files live.
3. Find your `grass.gdshader` file, click it, and **drag and drop** it directly into that empty **Shader** slot in the Inspector.

---

### Step 5: Add the Textures
As soon as you drop the shader in, the Inspector will instantly update and show a new section below called **Shader Parameters**. 
1. Unfold **Shader Parameters**. You will see slots for `noise_variation_1`, `noise_variation_2`, and `wind_noise`.
2. Drag whatever noise textures you want to use from your FileSystem dock right into those slots.

Once the textures are dropped in, the black color or default look will instantly update in your 3D viewport, and you'll see the shader working.

## Gemini:

> 7/20/2026 9:19:46

Creating a dedicated scene for the yard is a smart move, especially on your current hardware setup. Keeping the exterior environment separate from the interior apartment geometry prevents Godot from having to render everything at once, which will keep your frame rates much higher.

Here is the best way to handle this:

### Why a Separate Yard Scene Works Best
*   **Performance Isolation:** By making the yard its own scene (e.g., `YardEnvironment.tscn`), you can handle the heavy grass scattering, terrain meshes, and exterior lighting independently without cluttering your core architecture script.
*   **Clean Instancing:** You can simply instance your `ApartmentBuilder` scene directly into the yard scene, or use a portal/door trigger to transition between them.
*   **Target for the Grass Shader:** This new scene gives you a clean, dedicated ground mesh to apply that stylized grass material override to, without accidentally texturing your apartment floors with grass.

### How to Set It Up Right Now
1.  Click **Scene -> New Scene** at the very top of the Godot editor.
2.  In the Scene panel, choose **3D Scene** to create a `Node3D` root. Rename that root node to `Yard`.
3.  Save it immediately as `YardEnvironment.tscn`.
4.  Add a `MeshInstance3D` or `MultiMeshInstance3D` to this scene to act as your terrain/grass layer. *This* is the node where you will apply the `grass.gdshader` steps we mapped out.

## Gemini:

> 7/20/2026 9:22:53

That makes perfect sense, Scott. Seamlessly seeing the yard through that new living room door keeps the immersion intact, and planning for diggable areas later is an awesome feature for a hard sci-fi or horror setup. 

Since you want a seamless look on your current hardware configuration, we need to build the yard scene so it can be dynamically instanced or placed right alongside your apartment without killing your frame rate.

### Step 1: Create the Yard Scene Asset
1. Click **Scene -> New Scene** at the top of the editor.
2. Select **3D Scene** as the root node and rename it to `Yard`.
3. Save it as `Yard.tscn`.
4. Right-click the `Yard` root node, select **Add Child Node**, and add a **MeshInstance3D**. Rename it to `YardFloor`.
5. In the Inspector on the right, set its **Mesh** property to a **New PlaneMesh** and size it to cover the space outside the living room door (e.g., $X = 20, Z = 20$). *This is the mesh where you will apply the grass shader steps from earlier.*

---

### Step 2: Combine the Scenes Seamlessly
To see the yard through the window and door without loading screens, you just need to drop it into your main game loop scene:
1. Open your **Main Game Scene** (the one that runs when you hit play).
2. Click the **Chain Link Icon** (Instance Child Scene) at the top of the Scene Tree panel.
3. Select `Yard.tscn`. 
4. Use the transform arrows in the 3D viewport to slide the yard mesh right up against the exterior right wall of your apartment, aligning it with the outside door.

---

### Step 3: Preparing for Diggable Areas Later
To make sure you don't have to rewrite your whole scene layout when you add digging mechanics down the road, set it up right now using **GridMap** or a split mesh approach:
* **The Mesh Split Strategy:** Instead of one giant plane for the yard floor, break your yard into a grid of smaller square meshes (e.g., $2 \times 2$ meters each). 
* **Why this works for digging:** When the player uses a shovel tool later, your script can detect exactly which small square mesh they are standing on, swap its texture to a "dug dirt" material, or lower its $Y$ position to create a physical hole without disrupting the rest of the yard geometry.

## Gemini:

> 7/20/2026 9:33:04

Two issues sneaked into this script during the copy-paste process that will break normal walking:

1. **Missing standard movement execution:** In `_physics_process`, the `else:` block that runs `_process_standard_movement` when noclip is turned off got accidentally deleted[cite: 5, 8].
2. **Typo in the function name:** The standard movement function is declared as `_process_sstandard_movement` (with a double "s")[cite: 5, 8].

Here is the corrected drop-in replacement code for your `PlayerController.gd` script. It patches those errors and keeps the real-time grass trampling data routing intact[cite: 5, 8].

`​`​`gdscript
extends CharacterBody3D

@export var walk_speed: float = 4.0
@export var run_speed: float = 7.0
@export var jump_velocity: float = 4.5
@export var mouse_sensitivity: float = 0.002

# Noclip & Crouch variables
var is_noclip: bool = false
@export var noclip_speed: float = 10.0
var is_crouching: bool = false
var normal_height: float = 1.8
var crouch_height: float = 0.9

# Gravity from project settings
var gravity: float = ProjectSettings.get_setting("physics/3d/default_gravity")

@onready var head = $HeadAssembly
@onready var camera = $HeadAssembly/PlayerCamera
@onready var collision = $PlayerCollision

func _ready() -> void:
	Input.mouse_mode = Input.MOUSE_MODE_CAPTURED

func _unhandled_input(event: InputEvent) -> void:
	# ESCAPE: Toggle mouse lock/release for developer safety
	if event.is_action_pressed("ui_cancel") or (event is InputEventKey and event.keycode == KEY_ESCAPE and event.pressed):
		if Input.mouse_mode == Input.MOUSE_MODE_CAPTURED:
			Input.mouse_mode = Input.MOUSE_MODE_VISIBLE
		else:
			Input.mouse_mode = Input.MOUSE_MODE_CAPTURED
			
	# V KEY: Toggle Noclip mode
	if event is InputEventKey and event.keycode == KEY_V and event.pressed:
		is_noclip = !is_noclip
		collision.disabled = is_noclip
		velocity = Vector3.ZERO
		# Reset height if toggling out of noclip while crouching
		if not is_noclip:
			collision.shape.height = normal_height
		print("Noclip mode: ", "ON" if is_noclip else "OFF")

	# Mouse Look Logic
	if Input.mouse_mode == Input.MOUSE_MODE_CAPTURED and event is InputEventMouseMotion:
		rotate_y(-event.relative.x * mouse_sensitivity)
		head.rotate_x(-event.relative.y * mouse_sensitivity)
		head.rotation.x = clamp(head.rotation.x, deg_to_rad(-89), deg_to_rad(89))

func _physics_process(delta: float) -> void:
	if is_noclip:
		_process_noclip_movement(delta)
	else:
		_process_standard_movement(delta)
			
	# Send player position to the grass material for real-time trampling
	# Assumes your grass mesh node is named "YardFloor" inside the main parent context
	var grass_mesh = get_node_or_null("../YardFloor")
	if grass_mesh and grass_mesh.material_override:
		grass_mesh.material_override.set_shader_parameter("interracting_object_pos", global_position)

func _process_standard_movement(delta: float) -> void:
	# Add gravity
	if not is_on_floor():
		velocity.y -= gravity * delta

	# Handle Jump via Spacebar
	if Input.is_key_pressed(KEY_SPACE) and is_on_floor():
		velocity.y = jump_velocity

	# Handle Ducking via X Key
	if Input.is_key_pressed(KEY_X):
		is_crouching = true
		collision.shape.height = crouch_height
		head.position.y = 0.9 # Drop head assembly lower
	else:
		is_crouching = false
		collision.shape.height = normal_height
		head.position.y = 1.6 # Restore normal head height

	# Determine speed (Shift to run)
	var current_speed = run_speed if Input.is_key_pressed(KEY_SHIFT) else walk_speed
	if is_crouching:
		current_speed *= 0.5 # Slow down while ducking

	# Hardcoded WASD Vector Mapping
	var forward_back = 0.0
	var left_right = 0.0
	
	if Input.is_key_pressed(KEY_W): forward_back -= 1.0
	if Input.is_key_pressed(KEY_S): forward_back += 1.0
	if Input.is_key_pressed(KEY_A): left_right -= 1.0
	if Input.is_key_pressed(KEY_D): left_right += 1.0

	# Get input direction relative to player rotation
	var direction = (transform.basis * Vector3(left_right, 0, forward_back)).normalized()
	
	if direction:
		velocity.x = direction.x * current_speed
		velocity.z = direction.z * current_speed
	else:
		velocity.x = move_toward(velocity.x, 0, current_speed)
		velocity.z = move_toward(velocity.z, 0, current_speed)

	move_and_slide()

func _process_noclip_movement(delta: float) -> void:
	var forward_back = 0.0
	var left_right = 0.0
	
	if Input.is_key_pressed(KEY_W): forward_back -= 1.0
	if Input.is_key_pressed(KEY_S): forward_back += 1.0
	if Input.is_key_pressed(KEY_A): left_right -= 1.0
	if Input.is_key_pressed(KEY_D): left_right += 1.0
	
	# Compute direction based exactly on camera direction (3D flight vector)
	var direction = (camera.global_transform.basis * Vector3(left_right, 0, forward_back)).normalized()
	
	# Spacebar to fly UP, X or CTRL to fly DOWN
	var vertical_dir = 0.0
	if Input.is_key_pressed(KEY_SPACE):
		vertical_dir += 1.0
	if Input.is_key_pressed(KEY_X) or Input.is_key_pressed(KEY_CTRL):
		vertical_dir -= 1.0
		
	if direction or vertical_dir != 0.0:
		velocity = direction * noclip_speed
		velocity.y += vertical_dir * noclip_speed
	else:
		velocity = velocity.move_toward(Vector3.ZERO, noclip_speed * 0.2)
		
	# Global translation bypasses character body physics step entirely
	global_position += velocity * delta
`​`​`

Awesome that the main menu transition is working cleanly now, Scott. Let's get that load zone configured so stepping through the new outside door in the living room triggers the transition straight into your yard scene.

To do this without overcomplicating your file structure, we can add an interactive `Area3D` collision trigger box dynamically at the bottom of your `generate_apartment_layout()` function. This will act as the invisible boundary line right in the doorway.

### 1. Update `ApartmentBuilder.gd`

Scroll to the very bottom of your `generate_apartment_layout()` function in `ApartmentBuilder.gd` and paste this initialization block right before the closing print statement:

`​`​`gdscript
	# --- YARD EXIT PORTAL LOAD ZONE ---
	var load_zone = Area3D.new()
	load_zone.name = "YardLoadZone"
	# Positioned perfectly inside the living room outside door cutout frame (X=6.7, Z=2.0)
	load_zone.position = Vector3(6.6, 1.0, 2.0)
	
	var zone_collision = CollisionShape3D.new()
	var box_shape = BoxShape3D.new()
	box_shape.size = Vector3(0.6, 2.0, 1.4) # Slightly wider than the door frame to guarantee detection
	zone_collision.shape = box_shape
	
	load_zone.add_child(zone_collision)
	add_child(load_zone)
	
	# Connect the collision signal to fire when the character walks through
	load_zone.body_entered.connect(_on_yard_zone_entered)
	print("Load Zone Initialized: Yard transition trigger active at door boundary.")
`​`​`

Next, add the matching receiver function at the absolute bottom of the script (outside the generation function):

`​`​`gdscript
func _on_yard_zone_entered(body: Node) -> void:
	# Verify that it's actually the player walking through, not an NPC or object
	if body.name == "PlayerRoot":
		print("Player entered load zone. Switching context to Yard environment...")
		
		# Change this path to match your target yard scene file exactly
		var target_yard = "res://yard.tscn"
		if ResourceLoader.exists(target_yard):
			get_tree().change_scene_to_file(target_yard)
		else:
			printerr("Error: Could not locate scene file at: ", target_yard)
`​`​`

### Why this approach works:
* **Targeted Filtering:** Checking for `body.name == "PlayerRoot"` ensures your geometry collision logic only fires when your specific controller walks past the threshold.
* **Low Overhead:** Since it runs procedurally from your engine grid array scripts, you don't have to worry about manually linking signals across separate node files in the editor tree layout view.

## Gemini:

> 7/20/2026 9:58:42

That is a classic Godot safety catch. Because the engine is mid-calculation inside the physics loop when the player steps into the `Area3D`, it panics if you try to immediately wipe the current scene (and its collisions) out of memory. 

Moving the transition to an explicit **E** key interaction completely sidesteps this issue since it fires during standard input frame handling instead of a physics body update.

Here is the updated code to remove the automatic collision switch and replace it with a prompt that listens for an interaction press.

### 1. Update the Load Zone Setup in `ApartmentBuilder.gd`

Replace your previous `YardExitPortal` setup block at the bottom of `generate_apartment_layout()` with this version. It switches out the `body_entered` logic for tracking when the player is simply standing near the threshold:

`​`​`gdscript
	# --- YARD EXIT PORTAL INTERACTION ZONE ---
	var load_zone = Area3D.new()
	load_zone.name = "YardLoadZone"
	load_zone.position = Vector3(6.6, 1.0, 2.0)
	
	var zone_collision = CollisionShape3D.new()
	var box_shape = BoxShape3D.new()
	box_shape.size = Vector3(1.2, 2.0, 1.6) # Expanded slightly to give the player room to stand and press E
	zone_collision.shape = box_shape
	
	load_zone.add_child(zone_collision)
	add_child(load_zone)
	
	# Connect signals to monitor when the player is inside the interactable area
	load_zone.body_entered.connect(_on_yard_zone_entered)
	load_zone.body_exited.connect(_on_yard_zone_exited)
	
	# Add a tracking variable processing flag to the zone node dynamically
	load_zone.set_meta("player_inside", false)
	print("Interaction Zone Initialized: Approach door and press E to exit to Yard.")
`​`​`

---

### 2. Update the Interaction Functions

Replace your `_on_yard_zone_entered` function at the absolute bottom of `ApartmentBuilder.gd` and add the exit/input handling logic right below it:

`​`​`gdscript
func _on_yard_zone_entered(body: Node) -> void:
	if body.name == "PlayerRoot":
		var zone = get_node("YardLoadZone")
		if zone:
			zone.set_meta("player_inside", true)
			print("Prompt: Press 'E' to open door and step out into the yard.")

func _on_yard_zone_exited(body: Node) -> void:
	if body.name == "PlayerRoot":
		var zone = get_node("YardLoadZone")
		if zone:
			zone.set_meta("player_inside", false)

func _input(event: InputEvent) -> void:
	# Listen specifically for the E key press event
	if event is InputEventKey and event.keycode == KEY_E and event.pressed:
		var zone = get_node_or_null("YardLoadZone")
		if zone and zone.get_meta("player_inside") == true:
			_load_yard_scene()

func _load_yard_scene() -> void:
	print("Interacted. Safely shifting context to Yard environment...")
	var target_yard = "res://yard.tscn"
	if ResourceLoader.exists(target_yard):
		# call_deferred guarantees the engine finishes the current frame before loading the new scene
		get_tree().call_deferred("change_scene_to_file", target_yard)
	else:
		printerr("Error: Could not locate scene file at: ", target_yard)
`​`​`

### Why this fixes the crash:
* **`_input` context:** Pressing **E** runs inside the standard window processing stage rather than breaking into the narrow thread window where the physics server calculates structural overlaps.
* **`call_deferred` protection:** Using `call_deferred("change_scene_to_file", ...)` acts as a clean safety buffer, telling Godot to wait until the current processing frame finishes entirely before safely freeing the apartment geometry.

## Gemini:

> 7/20/2026 10:02:17

The screen is completely gray because `yard.tscn` loaded successfully, but it doesn't have a camera, player, or lighting setup inside it to view the environment[cite: 6]. It is only rendering the default engine sky void. The "E to interact" text also didn't display on-screen because we only printed it to the developer output console rather than a UI overlay node.

Let's fix both issues right now.

### 1. Fix the Gray Screen (Keep Player Alive)
Instead of wiping the entire scene out of memory and losing the player, we can instance the yard dynamically or simply carry the `PlayerRoot` node over into the new scene map. 

Update the `_load_yard_scene()` function at the bottom of `ApartmentBuilder.gd` to safely migrate the player structure:

`​`​`gdscript
func _load_yard_scene() -> void:
	print("Interacted. Safely shifting context to Yard environment...")
	var target_yard = "res://yard.tscn"
	if ResourceLoader.exists(target_yard):
		var player = get_node_or_null("PlayerRoot")
		if player:
			# Disconnect the player from the old map before freeing it
			player.get_parent().remove_child(player)
			
			var new_scene = load(target_yard).instantiate()
			get_tree().root.add_child(new_scene)
			get_tree().current_scene = new_scene
			
			# Drop the player into the new yard scene and reposition outside the doorway
			new_scene.add_child(player)
			player.global_position = Vector3(0.0, 1.0, 0.0) 
			
			queue_free() # Safely drop the old apartment data
	else:
		printerr("Error: Could not locate scene file at: ", target_yard)
`​`​`

### 2. Add the Visual "Press E" UI Popup
To see a physical text layout prompt appear on your monitor when you approach the doorway, add a simple canvas pop-up straight into the detection loop.

Replace the entrance/exit functions at the bottom of `ApartmentBuilder.gd` with this UI creation block:

`​`​`gdscript
var ui_prompt: Label = null

func _on_yard_zone_entered(body: Node) -> void:
	if body.name == "PlayerRoot":
		var zone = get_node("YardLoadZone")
		if zone:
			zone.set_meta("player_inside", true)
			
			# Instantiate a simple text overlay label canvas anchor
			if not ui_prompt:
				ui_prompt = Label.new()
				ui_prompt.text = "[ E ] EXIT TO YARD"
				ui_prompt.horizontal_alignment = HORIZONTAL_ALIGNMENT_CENTER
				ui_prompt.vertical_alignment = VERTICAL_ALIGNMENT_CENTER
				ui_prompt.set_anchors_preset(Control.PRESET_CENTER_BOTTOM)
				ui_prompt.grow_horizontal = Control.GROW_DIRECTION_BOTH
				ui_prompt.position.y -= 60 # Shift up slightly above the bottom boundary
				add_child(ui_prompt)

func _on_yard_zone_exited(body: Node) -> void:
	if body.name == "PlayerRoot":
		var zone = get_node("YardLoadZone")
		if zone:
			zone.set_meta("player_inside", false)
			if ui_prompt:
				ui_prompt.queue_free()
				ui_prompt = null
`​`​`

## Gemini:

> 7/20/2026 10:06:22

The "[ E ]" prompt appeared because our UI logic is tracking your position correctly, but the yard itself is still showing up as a grey void for a very specific reason: the `yard.tscn` scene currently has **no lighting or environment nodes** saved inside it[cite: 6, 7]. When Godot switches scenes, it unloads the apartment's light sources, leaving the new scene completely pitch black or default grey[cite: 6].

Let's fix this by updating `_load_yard_scene()` in `ApartmentBuilder.gd` to automatically inject a sun, sky environment, and camera tracking system the second you step out the door.

### The Fix

Open `ApartmentBuilder.gd` and replace the `_load_yard_scene()` function at the bottom with this version:

`​`​`gdscript
func _load_yard_scene() -> void:
	print("Interacted. Safely shifting context to Yard environment...")
	var target_yard = "res://yard.tscn"
	if ResourceLoader.exists(target_yard):
		var player = get_node_or_null("PlayerRoot")
		if player:
			# Disconnect player from old scene before freeing map memory
			player.get_parent().remove_child(player)
			
			var new_scene = load(target_yard).instantiate()
			get_tree().root.add_child(new_scene)
			get_tree().current_scene = new_scene
			
			# --- INJECT EMERGENCY OUTDOOR LIGHTING & SKY ---
			# Creates standard diagnostic sun lighting so the landscape isn't a grey black hole
			var sun = DirectionalLight3D.new()
			sun.position = Vector3(0, 10, 0)
			sun.rotation_degrees = Vector3(-45, 45, 0) # Angled down to cast clear shadows
			sun.light_energy = 1.0
			new_scene.add_child(sun)
			
			# Adds basic environment fallback settings so the background looks like open air
			var env_node = WorldEnvironment.new()
			var new_env = Environment.new()
			new_env.background_mode = Environment.BG_CLEAR_COLOR
			new_env.clear_color = Color(0.4, 0.5, 0.6) # Soft blue daylight horizon line tint
			env_node.environment = new_env
			new_scene.add_child(env_node)
			
			# Drop player into the yard and reset position safely at world coordinates
			new_scene.add_child(player)
			player.global_position = Vector3(0.0, 1.5, 0.0) 
			
			queue_free() # Clean old apartment data structures completely
	else:
		printerr("Error: Could not locate scene file at: ", target_yard)
`​`​`

### Why this clears up the void:
* **`DirectionalLight3D` Creation:** It generates a real-time global sun mesh right above the grass plane, forcing the 3D engine to render color values on your terrain surfaces.
* **`WorldEnvironment` Initialization:** It replaces the default dark grey fallback rendering background with a daytime horizon tint, proving instantly that the yard scene is alive.

## User:

> 7/20/2026 10:07:44

Invalid assignment of property or key 'clear_color' with value of type 'Color' on a base object of type 'Environment'.

## Gemini:

> 7/20/2026 10:07:44

Ah, that's my bad, Scott. In Godot 4, `clear_color` doesn't exist directly inside the `Environment` resource anymore—it's managed via the background settings or globally by the rendering server. Trying to set it directly causes it to crash out.

Let's fix it by setting the environment to use a background color cleanly. Open `ApartmentBuilder.gd` and swap out that **`# --- INJECT EMERGENCY OUTDOOR LIGHTING & SKY ---`** block inside `_load_yard_scene()` with this corrected code:

`​`​`gdscript
			# --- INJECT EMERGENCY OUTDOOR LIGHTING & SKY ---
			# Creates standard diagnostic sun lighting so the landscape isn't a grey black hole
			var sun = DirectionalLight3D.new()
			sun.position = Vector3(0, 10, 0)
			sun.rotation_degrees = Vector3(-45, 45, 0) # Angled down to cast clear shadows
			sun.light_energy = 1.0
			new_scene.add_child(sun)
			
			# Adds basic environment fallback settings so the background looks like open air
			var env_node = WorldEnvironment.new()
			var new_env = Environment.new()
			
			# Correct Godot 4 way to set a solid background color
			new_env.background_mode = Environment.BG_COLOR
			new_env.background_color = Color(0.4, 0.5, 0.6) # Soft blue daylight horizon line tint
			
			env_node.environment = new_env
			new_scene.add_child(env_node)
`​`​`

### What changed:
* **`Environment.BG_COLOR`**: Changed from `BG_CLEAR_COLOR` to `BG_COLOR` to tell the engine to use a custom solid color.
* **`background_color`**: Replaced the non-existent property with the correct parameter name. 

Give that a spin—it should let you press **E**, clear the apartment safely, and drop you straight onto your grass layout mesh under a daylight blue sky.

> 7/20/2026 10:10:11

That classic sequence timing error happens because we yanked the player out of the active scene tree using `remove_child()`, but then immediately tried to read their `global_position` on the very next line[cite: 8]. The moment a node is unparented, it doesn't exist anywhere in the 3D world, so asking for its global coordinates causes Godot to panic.

We just need to change the order: change the scene first, drop the player into the new map, and *then* update the position once they are back inside the scene tree[cite: 8].

Open `ApartmentBuilder.gd` and replace the `_load_yard_scene()` function with this ordered sequence:

`​`​`gdscript
func _load_yard_scene() -> void:
	print("Interacted. Safely shifting context to Yard environment...")
	var target_yard = "res://yard.tscn"
	if ResourceLoader.exists(target_yard):
		var player = get_node_or_null("PlayerRoot")
		if player:
			# 1. Safely remove the player from the old apartment layout
			player.get_parent().remove_child(player)
			
			# 2. Instantiate and establish the new yard scene
			var new_scene = load(target_yard).instantiate()
			get_tree().root.add_child(new_scene)
			get_tree().current_scene = new_scene
			
			# --- INJECT EMERGENCY OUTDOOR LIGHTING & SKY ---
			var sun = DirectionalLight3D.new()
			sun.position = Vector3(0, 10, 0)
			sun.rotation_degrees = Vector3(-45, 45, 0)
			sun.light_energy = 1.0
			new_scene.add_child(sun)
			
			var env_node = WorldEnvironment.new()
			var new_env = Environment.new()
			new_env.background_mode = Environment.BG_COLOR
			new_env.background_color = Color(0.4, 0.5, 0.6)
			env_node.environment = new_env
			new_scene.add_child(env_node)
			
			# 3. Add the player to the yard scene BEFORE touching positions
			new_scene.add_child(player)
			
			# 4. Now that the player is safely back inside the tree, update transform coordinates
			player.position = Vector3(0.0, 1.5, 0.0) 
			
			# 5. Obliterate the old apartment structural memory
			queue_free()
	else:
		printerr("Error: Could not locate scene file at: ", target_yard)
`​`​`

### Why this fixes the crash:
* By switching from `global_position` to local `position` right *after* running `add_child(player)`, the engine has a valid structural reference point to attach the player's spatial matrix to without throwing a null error.

## Gemini:

> 7/20/2026 10:11:11

The blue sky means the environment code is executing properly and the player node is successfully migrating into the new scene. The grass isn't showing up because the base `PlaneMesh` inside `yard.tscn` defaults to a tiny, flat $2\times2$ meter square with no visible vertices, and the `ShaderMaterial` needs explicit custom noise textures assigned to the uniforms to actually deform and display the stylized blades[cite: 6]. 

Without those texture inputs, the shader defaults to invisible or flat zero data values.

We can fix this by procedurally generating a high-quality noise setup right inside the scene migration block so it displays automatically without needing editor assignments.

Open `ApartmentBuilder.gd` and update the lower half of the `_load_yard_scene()` function where the yard properties are established:

`​`​`gdscript
			# 3. Add the player to the yard scene BEFORE touching positions
			new_scene.add_child(player)
			
			# 4. Now that the player is safely back inside the tree, update transform coordinates
			player.position = Vector3(0.0, 1.5, 0.0) 
			
			# --- PROCEDURALLY BUILD THE GRASS MATERIAL FOR ON-BOARD GPU ---
			var yard_floor = new_scene.get_node_or_null("YardFloor")
			if yard_floor:
				# Resize the mesh to give you a massive area to stand on
				var plane_mesh = PlaneMesh.new()
				plane_mesh.size = Vector2(50.0, 50.0)
				# Subdivide slightly so the vertex displacements have room to deform
				plane_mesh.subdivisions_width = 10
				plane_mesh.subdivisions_depth = 10
				yard_floor.mesh = plane_mesh
				
				# Generate localized FastNoiseLite textures dynamically to avoid setup lag
				var noise_tex1 = NoiseTexture2D.new()
				var noise_tex2 = NoiseTexture2D.new()
				var wind_tex = NoiseTexture2D.new()
				
				var base_noise = FastNoiseLite.new()
				base_noise.frequency = 0.05
				
				noise_tex1.noise = base_noise
				noise_tex2.noise = base_noise
				wind_tex.noise = base_noise
				
				# Assign the parameters directly to the ShaderMaterial override slot
				var mat = yard_floor.material_override as ShaderMaterial
				if mat:
					mat.set_shader_parameter("bottom_color", Color(0.05, 0.1, 0.05))
					mat.set_shader_parameter("top_color", Color(0.1, 0.25, 0.1))
					mat.set_shader_parameter("color_variation_1", Color(0.08, 0.2, 0.08))
					mat.set_shader_parameter("color_variation_2", Color(0.12, 0.3, 0.12))
					mat.set_shader_parameter("noise_variation_1", noise_tex1)
					mat.set_shader_parameter("noise_variation_2", noise_tex2)
					mat.set_shader_parameter("wind_noise", wind_tex)
			
			# 5. Obliterate the old apartment structural memory
			queue_free()
`​`​`

### Why this fixes the visual layout:
* **Mesh Expansion:** Changes the layout floor size from a microscopic point to a sprawling $50\times50$ meter zone.
* **Procedural Texturing:** Injects default system-generated noise textures into the shader parameter lines dynamically so the canvas renders terrain variations immediately.

Ah, my mistake again, Scott. In Godot 4, the property names for subdividing a `PlaneMesh` are `subdivisions_w` and `subdivisions_d` instead of the old full words. 

Let's fix that naming issue so it builds cleanly without crashing. Open up `ApartmentBuilder.gd` and look at the mesh setup lines inside `_load_yard_scene()`. Replace that specific mesh configuration block with this corrected one:

`​`​`gdscript
			# --- PROCEDURALLY BUILD THE GRASS MATERIAL FOR ON-BOARD GPU ---
			var yard_floor = new_scene.get_node_or_null("YardFloor")
			if yard_floor:
				# Resize the mesh to give you a massive area to stand on
				var plane_mesh = PlaneMesh.new()
				plane_mesh.size = Vector2(50.0, 50.0)
				
				# Correct Godot 4 property names for plane subdivisions
				plane_mesh.subdivisions_w = 10
				plane_mesh.subdivisions_d = 10
				yard_floor.mesh = plane_mesh
`​`​`

### What changed:
* **`subdivisions_w`**: Swapped from `subdivisions_width`.
* **`subdivisions_d`**: Swapped from `subdivisions_depth`.

That will fix the compilation error and get your grid topology ready for the grass shader to read.

func generate_apartment_layout() -> void:
	var building_root = CSGCombiner3D.new()
	building_root.use_collision = true
	building_root.set_collision_mask_value(1, true)
	add_child(building_root)

	ceiling_mat.roughness = 0.95
	
	# 1. Main Living Area Floor Plan Segment
	_add_plane(building_root, Vector3(2.6, 0.05, -1.0), Vector3(8.2, 0.1, 13.0), living_floor_mat)
	
	# 2. Kitchen Dedicated Floor Segment
	_add_plane(building_root, Vector3(2.6, 0.05, 6.6), Vector3(8.2, 0.1, 2.2), kitchen_floor_mat)
	
	# 3. Main Hallway and Bedrooms Core Base Flooring
	_add_plane(building_root, Vector3(-1.8, 0.05, -1.0), Vector3(4.2, 0.1, 13.4), living_floor_mat)
	
	# 4. Bathroom Isolated Floor Segment
	_add_plane(building_root, Vector3(-5.1, 0.05, 6.1), Vector3(3.2, 0.1, 3.0), bathroom_floor_mat)
	
	# Absolute ceiling bounds
	_add_plane(building_root, Vector3(0.0, 2.95, 0.0), Vector3(14.0, 0.1, 16.0), ceiling_mat)
	
	# Ground floors and ceilings layout duplicate bake
	_add_plane(building_root, Vector3(0.0, 0.05, 0.0), Vector3(14.0, 0.1, 16.0), floor_mat)
	_add_plane(building_root, Vector3(0.0, 2.95, 0.0), Vector3(14.0, 0.1, 16.0), ceiling_mat)

	var glass_mat = _load_horror_material("window01.png", Color(0.05, 0.1, 0.15), Vector3(1, 1, 1), 0.2)

	# --- DYNAMIC GALLERY WALL GENERATION ---
	for wall_data in wall_gallery:
		var current_mat = _load_horror_material(wall_data["texture"], Color(0.2, 0.2, 0.2))
		
		var wall = CSGBox3D.new()
		wall.position = wall_data["pos"]
		wall.size = wall_data["dims"]
		wall.material = current_mat
		building_root.add_child(wall)
		
		if wall_data.get("has_door", false):
			var door_cut = CSGBox3D.new()
			door_cut.operation = CSGShape3D.OPERATION_SUBTRACTION
			door_cut.position = wall_data["door_pos"]
			door_cut.size = wall_data["door_dims"]
			building_root.add_child(door_cut)
			
		if wall_data.get("has_window", false):
			_punch_absolute_window(building_root, wall_data["window_pos"], wall_data["window_dims"], glass_mat)

		{"name": "Living_Room_Light",  "pos": Vector3(4.0, 2.3, 1.0), "color": Color(0.95, 0.9, 0.8), "energy": 2.0, "range": 10.0},
		{"name": "Kitchen_Light",      "pos": Vector3(4.0, 2.3, 6.5), "color": Color(0.9, 0.95, 1.0), "energy": 1.8, "range": 8.0}
	]

	for light_data in room_lights:
		var light = OmniLight3D.new()
		light.name = light_data["name"]
		light.position = light_data["pos"]
		light.light_color = light_data["color"]
		light.light_energy = light_data["energy"]
		light.omni_range = light_data["range"]
		light.shadow_enabled = true
		light.shadow_blur = 1.5
		add_child(light)
		
	# --- YARD EXIT PORTAL INTERACTION ZONE ---
	var load_zone = Area3D.new()
	load_zone.name = "YardLoadZone"
	load_zone.position = Vector3(6.6, 1.0, 2.0)
	
	var zone_collision = CollisionShape3D.new()
	var box_shape = BoxShape3D.new()
	box_shape.size = Vector3(1.2, 2.0, 1.6)
	zone_collision.shape = box_shape
	
	load_zone.add_child(zone_collision)
	add_child(load_zone)
	
	load_zone.body_entered.connect(_on_yard_zone_entered)
	load_zone.body_exited.connect(_on_yard_zone_exited)
	load_zone.set_meta("player_inside", false)
	print("Interaction Zone Initialized: Approach door and press E to exit to Yard.")

func _on_yard_zone_entered(body: Node) -> void:
	if body.name == "PlayerRoot":
		var zone = get_node("YardLoadZone")
		if zone:
			zone.set_meta("player_inside", true)
			if not ui_prompt:
				ui_prompt = Label.new()
				ui_prompt.text = "[ E ] EXIT TO YARD"
				ui_prompt.horizontal_alignment = HORIZONTAL_ALIGNMENT_CENTER
				ui_prompt.vertical_alignment = VERTICAL_ALIGNMENT_CENTER
				ui_prompt.set_anchors_preset(Control.PRESET_CENTER_BOTTOM)
				ui_prompt.grow_horizontal = Control.GROW_DIRECTION_BOTH
				ui_prompt.position.y -= 60
				add_child(ui_prompt)

func _on_yard_zone_exited(body: Node) -> void:
	if body.name == "PlayerRoot":
		var zone = get_node("YardLoadZone")
		if zone:
			zone.set_meta("player_inside", false)
			if ui_prompt:
				ui_prompt.queue_free()
				ui_prompt = null

func _input(event: InputEvent) -> void:
	if event is InputEventKey and event.keycode == KEY_E and event.pressed:
		var zone = get_node_or_null("YardLoadZone")
		if zone and zone.get_meta("player_inside") == true:
			_load_yard_scene()

func _load_yard_scene() -> void:
	print("Interacted. Safely shifting context to Yard environment...")
	var target_yard = "res://yard.tscn"
	if ResourceLoader.exists(target_yard):
		var player = get_node_or_null("PlayerRoot")
		if player:
			player.get_parent().remove_child(player)
			
			var new_scene = load(target_yard).instantiate()
			get_tree().root.add_child(new_scene)
			get_tree().current_scene = new_scene
			
			# --- INJECT EMERGENCY OUTDOOR LIGHTING & SKY ---
			var sun = DirectionalLight3D.new()
			sun.position = Vector3(0, 10, 0)
			sun.rotation_degrees = Vector3(-45, 45, 0)
			sun.light_energy = 1.0
			new_scene.add_child(sun)
			
			var env_node = WorldEnvironment.new()
			var new_env = Environment.new()
			new_env.background_mode = Environment.BG_COLOR
			new_env.background_color = Color(0.4, 0.5, 0.6)
			env_node.environment = new_env
			new_scene.add_child(env_node)
			
			new_scene.add_child(player)
			player.position = Vector3(0.0, 1.5, 0.0) 
			
			# --- PROCEDURALLY BUILD THE GRASS MATERIAL FOR ON-BOARD GPU ---
			var yard_floor = new_scene.get_node_or_null("YardFloor")
			if yard_floor:
				var plane_mesh = PlaneMesh.new()
				plane_mesh.size = Vector2(50.0, 50.0)
				plane_mesh.subdivisions_w = 10
				plane_mesh.subdivisions_d = 10
				yard_floor.mesh = plane_mesh
				
				var noise_tex1 = NoiseTexture2D.new()
				var noise_tex2 = NoiseTexture2D.new()
				var wind_tex = NoiseTexture2D.new()
				
				var base_noise = FastNoiseLite.new()
				base_noise.frequency = 0.05
				
				noise_tex1.noise = base_noise
				noise_tex2.noise = base_noise
				wind_tex.noise = base_noise
				
				var mat = yard_floor.material_override as ShaderMaterial
				if mat:
					mat.set_shader_parameter("bottom_color", Color(0.05, 0.1, 0.05))
					mat.set_shader_parameter("top_color", Color(0.1, 0.25, 0.1))
					mat.set_shader_parameter("color_variation_1", Color(0.08, 0.2, 0.08))
					mat.set_shader_parameter("color_variation_2", Color(0.12, 0.3, 0.12))
					mat.set_shader_parameter("noise_variation_1", noise_tex1)
					mat.set_shader_parameter("noise_variation_2", noise_tex2)
					mat.set_shader_parameter("wind_noise", wind_tex)
			
			queue_free()
	else:
		printerr("Error: Could not locate scene file at: ", target_yard)

# --- ENGINE UTILITY HELPER FUNCTIONS ---

func _load_horror_material(file_name: String, fallback_color: Color, uv_scale: Vector3 = Vector3(1,1,1), rough: float = 0.95) -> Material:
	var mat = StandardMaterial3D.new()
	var full_path = "res://images/places/" + file_name
	if ResourceLoader.exists(full_path):
		mat.albedo_texture = load(full_path)
	else:
		mat.albedo_color = fallback_color
	mat.uv1_scale = uv_scale
	mat.roughness = rough
	return mat

func _add_plane(root: Node3D, pos: Vector3, dims: Vector3, mat: Material) -> void:
	var plane = CSGBox3D.new()
	plane.position = pos
	plane.size = dims
	plane.material = mat
	root.add_child(plane)

func _punch_absolute_window(root: Node3D, pos: Vector3, dims: Vector3, glass_mat: Material) -> void:
	var window_cut = CSGBox3D.new()
	window_cut.operation = CSGShape3D.OPERATION_SUBTRACTION
	window_cut.position = pos
	window_cut.size = dims
	root.add_child(window_cut)
	
	var glass_pane = CSGBox3D.new()
	glass_pane.position = pos
	glass_pane.size = Vector3(dims.x - 0.05, dims.y - 0.05, 0.05) if dims.x > dims.z else Vector3(0.05, dims.y - 0.05, dims.z - 0.05)
	glass_pane.material = glass_mat
	root.add_child(glass_pane)
`​`​`

## Gemini:

> 7/20/2026 10:26:09

In Godot 4, a `PlaneMesh` does not actually use `subdivisions_w` or `subdivisions_d` as direct properties. The correct properties for a `PlaneMesh` are **`subdivisions_width`** and **`subdivisions_depth`**. 

The initial error happened because the script was trying to assign an integer to the mesh before the dynamic texture or material resource architecture was fully instantiated on the node.

Let's clean this up so the `PlaneMesh` geometry initializes correctly without failing. Replace the `# --- PROCEDURALLY BUILD THE GRASS MATERIAL FOR ON-BOARD GPU ---` block inside `_load_yard_scene()` with this version:

`​`​`gdscript
			# --- PROCEDURALLY BUILD THE GRASS MATERIAL FOR ON-BOARD GPU ---
			var yard_floor = new_scene.get_node_or_null("YardFloor")
			if yard_floor:
				var plane_mesh = PlaneMesh.new()
				plane_mesh.size = Vector2(50.0, 50.0)
				
				# Explicit Godot 4 properties for PlaneMesh geometry subdivision
				plane_mesh.subdivisions_width = 10
				plane_mesh.subdivisions_depth = 10
				yard_floor.mesh = plane_mesh
				
				# Generate localized FastNoiseLite textures dynamically to avoid setup lag
				var noise_tex1 = NoiseTexture2D.new()
				var noise_tex2 = NoiseTexture2D.new()
				var wind_tex = NoiseTexture2D.new()
				
				var base_noise = FastNoiseLite.new()
				base_noise.frequency = 0.05
				
				noise_tex1.noise = base_noise
				noise_tex2.noise = base_noise
				wind_tex.noise = base_noise
				
				# Safely extract or generate the material resource interface
				var mat = yard_floor.material_override as ShaderMaterial
				if not mat:
					mat = ShaderMaterial.new()
					# Check if the global resource exists before parsing path strings
					if ResourceLoader.exists("res://grass.gdshader"):
						mat.shader = load("res://grass.gdshader")
					yard_floor.material_override = mat
				
				if mat and mat.shader:
					mat.set_shader_parameter("bottom_color", Color(0.05, 0.1, 0.05))
					mat.set_shader_parameter("top_color", Color(0.1, 0.25, 0.1))
					mat.set_shader_parameter("color_variation_1", Color(0.08, 0.2, 0.08))
					mat.set_shader_parameter("color_variation_2", Color(0.12, 0.3, 0.12))
					mat.set_shader_parameter("noise_variation_1", noise_tex1)
					mat.set_shader_parameter("noise_variation_2", noise_tex2)
					mat.set_shader_parameter("wind_noise", wind_tex)
`​`​`

### What This Fixes
* **Property Alignment:** Restores `subdivisions_width` and `subdivisions_depth`, which are the exact parameters the engine expects for building custom procedural topology arrays on a `PlaneMesh`.
* **Material Safety Fallback:** Added a fallback check (`if not mat:`) so that if the `ShaderMaterial` slot is completely unassigned inside your `yard.tscn` file, it will instantiate a new material instance at runtime and load the grass shader code directly.

Because the original `yard.tscn` file has a default `PlaneMesh` with a size of only $2 \times 2$ meters, scaling it by $25\times$ turns it into a flat surface, but it might still be invisible if the shader has no fallback compilation data[cite: 6].

### 1. The Safe Yard Blueprint Fix
Let's modify `_load_yard_scene()` inside `ApartmentBuilder.gd` to ensure the floor is physically visible, dropped down slightly so you aren't stuck inside the geometry, and lit properly.

Replace the inner segment of the `# --- PROCEDURALLY BUILD THE GRASS MATERIAL FOR ON-BOARD GPU ---` code block inside `_load_yard_scene()` with this bulletproof visibility verification layout:

					debug_mat.roughness = 0.9

			# Ensure the player drops down slightly above the floor surface line
			player.position = Vector3(0.0, 0.2, 0.0)
`​`​`

### 2. Integrating the FOOTSTEPPER Plugin
Since you have the **FOOTSTEPPER** plugin structural layout active in your directory, it generally relies on reading metadata or physics layers to know what ground material the character is traversing.

Based on the documentation for your **FOOTSTEPPER** plugin, we don't need to manually calculate the physics slide vectors or pull metadata strings in our movement script[cite: 10]. The plugin has an **Automatic Mode** that does the heavy lifting for you by running its own downward-facing raycast[cite: 10]. 

Because your apartment layout is built using procedurally generated `CSGBox3D` meshes and the yard is a flat `MeshInstance3D`, the absolute cleanest way to integrate this is to use **Method 3b** from the plugin instructions: dropping a `FootstepperTag` node directly into the yard geometry[cite: 10].

Here is how to wire it up right now.

---

### Step 1: Update the Yard Generation Loop
Open `ApartmentBuilder.gd` and look at the bottom of the `_load_yard_scene()` function where the `YardFloor` is processed[cite: 9]. We will add a block that dynamically creates and attaches a `FootstepperTag` node straight to the ground mesh[cite: 10].

Update that segment inside `_load_yard_scene()` to look like this:

`​`​`gdscript
			# --- PROCEDURALLY BUILD THE GRASS MATERIAL FOR ON-BOARD GPU ---
			var yard_floor = new_scene.get_node_or_null("YardFloor")
			if yard_floor:
				# Scale the node directly to make the yard massive 
				yard_floor.scale = Vector3(50.0, 1.0, 50.0) 
				
				# --- FOOTSTEPPER PLUGIN TAG INTEGRATION ---
				# Instead of raw metadata, we instantiate the specific tag class the plugin expects
				var footstep_tag = Node.new()
				footstep_tag.set_script(load("res://addons/footstepper/footstepper_tag.gd")) 
				footstep_tag.name = "FootstepperTag"
				# Set the specific surface string name to match your sound profile file configurations
				footstep_tag.set("material_name", "grass") 
				yard_floor.add_child(footstep_tag)
				
				# Generate localized FastNoiseLite textures dynamically to avoid setup lag
				var noise_tex1 = NoiseTexture2D.new()
`​`​`

---

### Step 2: Clean Up `PlayerController.gd`
Since the plugin handles the audio calculations automatically when nested inside a `CharacterBody3D`, you can remove any manual vector tracking checks we added to `PlayerController.gd`[cite: 10]. Your `_process_standard_movement` function should just conclude with its native layout[cite: 8]:

`​`​`gdscript
	# Get input direction relative to player rotation
	var direction = (transform.basis * Vector3(left_right, 0, forward_back)).normalized()
	
	if direction:
		velocity.x = direction.x * current_speed
		velocity.z = direction.z * current_speed
	else:
		velocity.x = move_toward(velocity.x, 0, current_speed)
		velocity.z = move_toward(velocity.z, 0, current_speed)

	move_and_slide()
`​`​`

---

### Step 3: Configure the Scene Node Tree
To ensure the automated raycast can read the floor surfaces instantly, make sure the `Footstepper` node is configured properly inside your editor setup:

1. Open `apartment.tscn` in the editor[cite: 1].
2. Right-click your `PlayerRoot` node (`CharacterBody3D`) and select **Add Child Node**[cite: 1, 10].
3. Search for and add the **`Footstepper`** node[cite: 10].
4. In the Inspector panel on the right side for the `Footstepper` node[cite: 10]:
   * Toggle **Material Aware** to **True**[cite: 10].
   * Set the **Collision Mask** to include Layer 1 (matching your structure layouts)[cite: 2, 10].
   * Slide the `Footstepper` node's translation layout down towards the bottom of the capsule boundaries so its 1.25m raycast hits the floor cleanly[cite: 1, 10].
   * Add a profile item inside the `sound_profiles` array named `grass` and drop your audio clip files right into it[cite: 10].

func _ready() -> void:
	generate_apartment_layout()

func generate_apartment_layout() -> void:
	var building_root = CSGCombiner3D.new()
	building_root.use_collision = true
	building_root.set_collision_mask_value(1, true)
	add_child(building_root)
	
	var living_floor_mat = _load_horror_material("floor1.png", Color(0.1, 0.07, 0.05), Vector3(6.0, 6.0, 6.0), 0.85)
	var floor_mat = living_floor_mat
	var kitchen_floor_mat = _load_horror_material("kittile.png", Color(0.2, 0.2, 0.2), Vector3(4.0, 4.0, 4.0), 0.5) 
	var bathroom_floor_mat = _load_horror_material("bathtile.png", Color(0.25, 0.25, 0.25), Vector3(3.0, 3.0, 3.0), 0.4)
